# SCRIPTS.md — Script Lifecycle Registry

> This file is the Single Source of Truth (Tier 1 SSOT) for every TypeScript script in `scripts/` (workspace root). The registry covers all `.ts` files: top-level CLI scripts run via `bun scripts/<name>.ts` or `bun run <alias>`, as well as internal modules under `scripts/lib/`, `scripts/helpers/`, `scripts/hooks/`, `scripts/validators/`, and `scripts/experiments/` that are imported by other scripts or wired as hooks rather than invoked standalone. These modules are registered here for lifecycle and version tracking even though they have no independent CLI usage.
> Template `templates/common/scripts/` (Tier 2) is a snapshot published from here via `bun run propagate:apply`.
> Project `scripts/` (Tier 3) is a snapshot created from Tier 2 at `new-project` time.
>
> **Machine parsing**: `verify-scripts.ts --verify` reads the `## Registry` section only.
> **Human reading**: see `## Guide` section below for purpose, usage, and deprecation notes.

---

## Architecture: TypeScript-Only Policy (ADR-0036)

> **Policy change (2026-06-11)**: All scripts are TypeScript executed via Bun. The former Tier 1 sh/ps1 bootstrap tier has been abolished. See [ADR-0036](../docs/adr/0036-script-ts-migration.md) for rationale.

## Error Handling Standard (ADR-0054)

> **Policy (2026-08-16)**: Error and exit paths in all scripts MUST use `scripts/lib/error-handling.ts` (`die()`, `fatalError()` + `logError()`, `withSyncErrorHandling()`). Migration is **incremental** — a script migrates when it is otherwise modified (no pure-consistency rewrites). When migrating an L0+L1 script, sync the change to `templates/common/scripts/` in the same commit. See [ADR-0054](../docs/adr/0054-error-handling-standardization.md) and `docs/constitution/08-coding-guidelines.md` §8.11.

All scripts in this workspace are written in TypeScript and executed via `bun`. There is no longer a distinction between "bootstrap" and "ops" tiers — Bun is a hard prerequisite for the workspace and is assumed to be installed before any script runs.

**Single rule**: every new script must be a `.ts` file. No `.sh` or `.ps1` files will be accepted.

**Invocation pattern**:
```bash
bun scripts/<name>.ts [args]       # direct
bun run <alias>                     # via package.json alias (preferred for CI)
```

### Ops & Automation Scripts (Bun/TypeScript)
*   **Purpose**: All scripting tasks — project scaffolding, pipeline, code generation, linting, syncing, lifecycle audits.
*   **Implementation**: Written in TypeScript (`.ts`), executed via the Bun runtime.
*   **Execution**: `bun scripts/<name>.ts` or via `package.json` alias.
*   **Examples**: `upgrade-project.ts`, `cleanup-completed-md.ts`, `audit.ts`, `dev-sync.ts`.

---

## Registry Scope

The registry below intentionally covers top-level `scripts/*.ts` entry points only. The following are deliberately **outside** the registry (helper/internal scope, not standalone lifecycle-managed scripts):

- `scripts/helpers/plugins/*` — plugin modules consumed by registered scripts
- `scripts/helpers/registries/*` — registry data modules loaded by tooling
- `scripts/hooks/_test-*.ts` — internal test fixtures for hook scripts

Their absence from the table is policy-consistent, not an oversight.

Every project's report gains a zero-inclusive `tombstone census` line (honored / unconfirmed) so the daily fleet report shows the census row for every project. loadDeliveryTombstoneState exported for unit tests. Prior: v2.7.0 (2026-11-25, T-20261124-001 — design docs/designs/2026-11-25-upgrade-runner-machinery-batch-design.md §4.1): the §13 root-backfill staleness key is `graphContentSha256` — the SHA-256 of docs/skill-graph.json as stored at the graph commit, read with `git show <commit>:<pathspec>` — recorded beside `graphHeadSha` (kept as information, compared only when no content hash is available). Freshness no longer depends on the order of two commits: dev-sync regenerates the graph while landing, so the graph commit moved again after the artifact was written although the content was equal, and the next fleet-report run rebuilt and dirtied the tree. A file without the field is stale once. v2.6.2 (2026-11-24, backfill invalidation-key fix): the §13 root-backfill staleness key is `graphHeadSha` — the last commit that touched the replayed graph path (`git log -1 --format=%H -- <pathspec>`, POSIX-shaped via `toGitPath()`, exposed as `readGraphHeadSha`) — instead of the repo HEAD. Keying on HEAD was a fixed-point violation: merging/committing the artifact advanced HEAD, invalidated the file on the next run, dirtied the tree, and emitted another PR (#2162 → #2164 → #2165 loop). A merge of the artifact does not touch `docs/skill-graph.json`, so the second run after a merge skips the rebuild and leaves a clean tree (convergence). The `RootBackfillFile`/`BackfillSummary`/structure-model field is renamed `headSha` → `graphHeadSha`; a legacy file carrying only `headSha` lacks `graphHeadSha` and reads as stale exactly once, then stabilizes. Both page provenance notes now read "as of the last commit that changed the skill graph". Supersedes closed PR #2165. Prior: v2.6.1 (2026-11-23, Windows CI fix): the §13 root-backfill git operands (`git log -- <pathspec>` and `git show <sha>:<path>`) are POSIX-shaped via `toGitPath()` — a `path.join`-built native path made git reject the pathspec on Windows ("path 'docs\skill-graph.json' exists on disk, but not in <sha>"), failing 3 backfill tests on the Windows leg; the recorded backfill `path` field is normalized to the same git form (cross-platform byte-identical artifact). Regression pinned by `toGitPath` unit assertions plus a backslash-shaped `replayGraphHistory` call in tests/unit/skill-graph-observability.test.ts. Prior: v2.6.0 (2026-11-23, spec docs/designs/2026-11-23-skill-graph-observability-design.md §5+§13): skill-graph observability extension — additive optional per-project `agentGraph` snapshot block (agent id+hash table + deduped agent-touching edge tuples in index form over a per-project edge-type enum; legacy snapshots without it render "tracking begins" tolerance notes; every pre-existing field byte-value-identical, added ≈42-65KB on the current fleet); per-date agent-node + per-edge-type NEW/VANISHED diff classes computed at render by replaying consecutive snapshots (20-sample caps, server-computed relative links to the snapshot files); sixth **Agents** tab (agents per project, agent↔skill relationship counts by edge type, newest relationship changes, ≥3-project convergence rollup mirroring the analytics capability rules — report-only, the fleet report never files tickets) plus a small link card to the structure page (≤2KB); the same run emits `docs/skill-graph-structure.html` (rides the existing --html/--no-html family — NO new CLI flags; independent WARN island): layer composition + hubs as no-JS inline SVG, full-root ego explorer with labeled edge types (~130KB vs the 800KB budget), per-variant root/template/project juxtaposition with per-hop layer vocabulary; §13 addendum: deterministic bounded root git-history backfill emitting `memory/skill-graph-metrics/root-backfill.json` (third WARN island; skips when the recorded headSha matches `git rev-parse HEAD`; 1,000-commit replay cap aborts with a WARN; ~1.6MB compact at the 340-commit corpus, replay ≈2.3s; consumed by both pages — commit-granularity root timeline with a visible "backfilled from git history" provenance marker). Snapshot-filename contract untouched (freshness gate invisible). Pinned by tests/unit/skill-graph-observability.test.ts. Prior: v2.5.0 (2026-11-23, spec docs/designs/2026-11-23-skill-graph-history-report-design.md §5.1-§5.3): auto-emitted HTML history dashboard — every successful report run (markdown or `--json`; the daily runner's `--json` invocation is the primary emitter) regenerates a single self-contained page over the full snapshot history at `docs/skill-graph-history.html` after the snapshot write (`--html <path>` overrides with parent auto-creation, `--no-html` skips; emission failure is exactly one stderr WARN — exit code and snapshot never affected; output is deterministic — no wall-clock markers — so reruns are byte-identical; ≤300 KB at the current corpus). The page inlines a compact projection (per-date convergence class counts + NEW/VANISHED pairwise replay, derived gap bands, latest convergence/delivery-gap/fan-in/orphan detail, dynamic fleet coverage with transient-PR-checkout annotation, governance-ticket scan) as a `\u003c`-escaped JSON data block rendered client-side — five tabs, in-page search, light/dark toggle, CSP `default-src 'none'` + unsafe-inline style/script, zero external references. Governance-ticket reader implements folded (`>-`) / literal-block / plain / quoted top-level scalars over `tickets/governance/*.yaml` + `archive/` (id from field or filename; `skill-graph:`-prefixed titles badged runner-filed). Byte-compat: snapshot writes and `--json` stdout unchanged from v2.4.0. Pinned by tests/unit/skill-graph-fleet-report-html.test.ts. Transient codes (LOCK_TRANSIENT {EBUSY, EPERM, EACCES, EAGAIN} — EACCES added per the ticket, the rest parity with the in-file precedent) on the lock-dir mkdir are now a FAILED ACQUISITION inside the existing deadline loop: logged once per code, Bun.sleepSync(25), retried until the UPSTREAM_LOCK_TIMEOUT_MS deadline (default 5000ms), whose expiry throws IntakeLockTimeoutError carrying the LAST transient code in the message ('... last fs error EPERM on .intake-lock mkdir') so diagnosis is one read away; FAIL-CLOSED unchanged (v1.8.0 M5 — still-failing after the deadline still answers retryable -32000, never a silent unlocked fallthrough). mkdirSync(LOGS_DIR) wraps withFsRetry(3, 'lock logs dir', LOCK_TRANSIENT) — withFsRetry gained an optional transient-set parameter defaulting to TRANSIENT_FS, so existing call sites keep v1.10.1 semantics untouched. T-id status returns the narrow kanban field set only (id, title, status, triage when present, priority, sev when set, delegation: true — no result/error content), by explicit id only (unfiltered listings stay U-only and project-scoped), not-found semantics identical to the U-path, self_declared callers keep the read-only posture. T-id reprioritize rides the same store reprioritizeTicket enforcement (spoke caps +1 level per 24h, P0 user-only, 3-strike ledger, history[] append, shared ticket lock); the U-path's upstream.project ownership check cannot apply to T-tickets (delegation tickets carry no per-project owner metadata — recorded as a known gap in ADR-0103; every call is audit-logged with the gateway-derived spoke-pm:<project> actor). No new write surface: spokes file T-tickets via ticket.ts (read + reprioritize only). Prior: v1.51.0 (2026-11-18, T-20261117-015 — spec docs/designs/2026-11-18-dev-sync-hunk-staging-design.md §2-§3): two strictly additive mechanisms for hunk-level adjudicated landings (the 2026-11-17 evidence chain: three manual hunk-edit stagings in one day, one silent-clobber landing incident, memory/2026-11-17.md via PR #2073). (1) HONOR-STAGED OPT-IN — `--honor-staged <path>` (repeatable) and `SYNC_HONOR_STAGED` (comma-separated) designate paths whose PRE-STAGED index blob is authoritative: the step-6.5 promotion sweep excludes honored paths from every `git add` batch (the same "the index already holds the desired state" contract as the staged-deletion skip), so a hunk-adjudicated partial docs/specs/registry.json survives the run instead of being silently clobbered by the path-level re-add; the worktree-landing pattern (git apply --cached, then sync) becomes first-class — no worktree reconstruction, no shelved-unshelved dance. Fail-closed: an honored path with NO staged delta is a ghost opt-in → FATAL (repair: re-apply the adjudicated hunks, re-sync); honor under --warn-staging is FATAL (the git add -A soak cannot honor anything); worktree-vs-index divergence on an honored path is a visible WARN (normal adjudication shape — the worktree keeps the sibling batch for a later sync). (2) STEP 6.55 SPEC-REGISTRY DANGLING-REFERENCE GATE — when docs/specs/registry.json is landing in THIS commit, every entry\u0027s entry JSON (docs/specs/entries/<id>.json) and its design `file` target must exist in the resulting commit (a blob in the index, or a blob in HEAD not removed by this landing — parseCachedNameStatus); any dangling reference is FATAL before git commit and a malformed staged registry fails closed. The 2026-11-17 incident\u0027s actual bite: the forbidden entry rode the commit as a dangling reference past every pre-commit gate — only push-time review caught it. Pure detection: lib/spec-landing.ts v1.0.0 (one derivation, two callers; unit-pinned by tests/unit/spec-landing.test.ts + tests/unit/spec-landing-incident-sim.test.ts; contract-pinned by tests/unit/dev-sync-hunk-staging.test.ts). Prior: v1.50.0 (2026-11-17, U-20261116-012 — spec docs/designs/2026-11-17-root-machinery-batch-design.md §2, DEC-20261117-03): two re-run-hygiene defects. (1) ignore-aware promotion staging — a plain `git add` refuses an explicitly named ignored path even when tracked (verified on git 2.56.1, the .zcode/config.json class; `.gitignore` negations are non-viable — a negation cannot re-include a file under an excluded parent), so the step-6.5 promotion sweep's batched add exited 1 and killed the whole sync; the sweep now classifies with two batched queries (`git ls-files -z`, `git check-ignore --no-index` in line mode — default check-ignore silently excludes TRACKED paths from its report (the exact class to find) and `-z` is only meaningful with `--stdin` and fatals otherwise, verified on git 2.56.1; exits 0/1/128 handled) and partitions: tracked∩ignored → `git add -f` with one DIM log line per path (tracked-status intent wins), untracked∩ignored → WARN + skip (never force-add scratch), everything else → the plain batch add unchanged; the absent-path branch applies the same rule (tracked∩ignored worktree deletion staged with `-f`, absent ignored-untracked keeps the ghost-path failure). (2) branch-keyed session-block replace — step 2's memory session block gains a leading `<!-- dev-sync:session branch=<branch> -->` marker and a same-branch same-day re-run replaces the marker-plus-block segment (through the next `\n---\n` boundary or EOF; stray same-branch duplicates collapse) instead of appending a near-duplicate: the v1.32.0 idempotency check keyed on the exact commit subject, which misses whenever the derived subject differs between attempts (the normal case — step 6.9 re-derives from the final staged set); different branch → separate block, no marker (legacy) → exact-subject skip + plain append unchanged; a replaced block carries its old `## Skills Used` section forward verbatim; block-start well-formedness accepts the leading marker comment (memory-union-verify v1.3.0, design §2.2 item 4). Unit-pinned by tests/unit/dev-sync-rerun-hygiene.test.ts (block-slice, B-T1..B-T8). Prior: v1.45.0 (2026-11-09, spec docs/designs/2026-11-08-gw-tenant-loop-and-hardening-design.md §11): repository-config integrity guard — (a) START-OF-RUN INVARIANT immediately after the CWD guard: assertWorkTreeHealthy() turns the cryptic "fatal: this operation must be run in a work tree" left by core.bare=true into one line naming the repair; (b) new BLOCKING step 4.88, after the last test-executing step (4.85) and before the 4.9 audit gate: the run-start local-config snapshot is compared against a post snapshot and ANY delta is FATAL — a spawned test mutated the repository config, and worktrees share the main .git/config so the damage is repo-wide. No key excluded (no sanctioned writer touches this repository config during a run). Primitives: lib/repo-integrity.ts v1.0.0. Prior: v1.44.2 (2026-11-09, P0 correctness fix): the two test spawns now run under `.env(gitCleanEnvForShell())` — a FULL process.env copy minus the redirection keys — because Bun Shell's `.env(obj)` REPLACES the child environment (verified on Bun 1.4.4: `.env({GIT_DIR: undefined})` leaves the child with an empty PATH). The v1.44.1 unset-map payload therefore ran `bun run test:unit` with no PATH and 26 of 268 unit files failed at step 4.85 with `Executable not found in $PATH: "git"`; proof and incident chain in scripts/lib/git-env.ts v1.2.0. v1.44.1 (2026-11-09, rogue-commit incident): the two test spawns — step 3.95 `bun run test` and step 4.85 `bun run test:unit` — ran under `.env(gitEnvUnset())` (mechanism RETRACTED in v1.44.2; it masked GIT_DIR but destroyed PATH), so a hook context's GIT_DIR never reaches the upgrade-project fixtures; that leak landed 10 rogue `Test <test@example.com>` commits on the PR branch (~393 files, three L1 core scripts deleted). v1.44.0 (2026-11-08, spec docs/designs/2026-11-08-gw-tenant-loop-and-hardening-design.md §5.1): new step 6.4 — fixture-root hygiene residue gate, BLOCKING, immediately before the 6.5 scoped-staging S1 snapshot (the design's proposed "Step 5.9", numbered 6.4 at edit time per §10 note-1 so it sits between the section-6 sensitive-file guard and the staging snapshot): an untracked WHOLE top-level directory (`?? Projects/`, `?? tests/`, …) at the repo root can only be test-fixture residue — the relative-mkdtemp-root class behind the 2026-11-04 `rm -rf Projects` incident (memory/2026-11-04.md:159, T-20261104-003/-004); PR #1855 made the fixtures absolute and this gate makes it enforced. Untracked FILES inside tracked dirs are ordinary work and stay with 6.5's residual classification; git failure is fail-closed. Classifier is the pure lib/workspace-hygiene.ts v1.0.0, shared with the standalone scripts/check-workspace-hygiene.ts (one derivation, two callers); structural placement pinned by tests/unit/workspace-hygiene.test.ts. Byte-identical overlap files stay residual (agent pre-run leftovers kept out); deletions keep the existing path. Completes design §5.3's "a repaired file passes" premise — the v1 implementation compared the merge commit's own blob, making forward repairs impossible (live-blocked since merge c8aba5d61). Prior: v1.40.0 (2026-11-05, T-20261104-001 — spec docs/designs/2026-11-05-merge-integrity-design.md §5.1): new step 3.88 — memory session-block union-verify, BLOCKING (two-tier rollout user-approved 2026-11-05): Arm A sweeps bounded recent merge commits (7 days AND 20 merges) touching flat memory logs via helpers/memory-union-verify.ts v1.0.0 (pure core, unit-pinned by tests/unit/memory-union-verify.test.ts, byte-identical L1 mirror; self-contained — node:fs + git subprocess, no guarded dynamic import unlike ticket-drift.ts) and requires every parent block to survive into the merged result (order/dedup-tolerant, heading+firstLine fallback for cosmetic reflows) plus structural well-formedness of the merged result; Arm B enforces the same structure on every flat memory log at HEAD (block count === separator count + 1, '## '-headed blocks, no block with >= 2 '## Session Summary' headings — the fused-separator signature from the #1842/#1845 incidents). Post-activation merges (GATE_3_88_ACTIVATED_AT = the gate landing instant) and all worktree findings are FATAL with repair guidance; pre-activation merge findings are WARN — the design's "expected pass on first run" premise was false (the window holds real immutable-history drops, e.g. merge 3db832974 / PR #1850 dropped ~20 session blocks) and those age out with the 7-day window; the five pre-existing malformed logs (2026-10-29/-30/-31, 11-02, 11-03) were structurally repaired in the landing commit. Absent memory/ = documented no-op. Prior: v1.39.0 (2026-11-05, T-20261104-004 item 3 — spec docs/designs/2026-11-04-project-loss-hardening-design.md §5.3b): new step 3.87 — ticket-store memory-drift check, BLOCKING: every T-/U- id cited in memory/*.md must resolve via helpers/ticket-drift.ts v1.0.0 (live stores + both archives); `ticket.ts doctor` carries the same check as the report-only diagnostic arm. Prior: v1.38.0 (2026-11-04, U-20261104-003): new step 4.87 — agent frontmatter last_updated bump. A sync pass that mechanically rewrites an agent file's YAML frontmatter (tier normalization, model-registry delivery, stub merges) leaves the file's top-level last_updated stale, so agent-lifecycle-audit emits identical staleness warnings for every touched file on every later run (co-newbiz: 34 warnings off the single 2026-10-28 sync commit 0b1a406c). After the pipeline's writes and before the audit gate, the pass inspects every sync-modified agent .md (agents/, templates/**/agents/) whose HEAD-to-worktree diff is confined to the frontmatter block and re-stamps the top-level last_updated to the run date via bumpFrontmatterLastUpdated (helpers/upgrade-versions.ts — no key injection, idempotent). Negative contracts hold by construction: body edits never touched; unchanged files never enter the modified set (no-op syncs stay no-ops); new files skipped. Decision core is the pure helpers/agent-mechanical-date-bump.ts v1.0.0 (unit-pinned by tests/unit/agent-mechanical-date-bump.test.ts). Non-blocking hygiene pass — individual failures WARN and the lifecycle gates still see any stale date that escapes. Prior: v1.37.0 (2026-10-31, U-20261029-004 — spec docs/designs/2026-10-31-variant-generators-design.md §4-§5): new step 3.86 — declared generator pre-convergence, joining the regeneration step family before the audit gate: runs every project-authored `scripts/<variant>/generators.json` manifest via scripts/lib/generator-manifest.ts v1.0.0 (array-form Bun.spawnSync, argv[0] must be `bun`, cwd pinned to the project root, no env fabrication, ≤ 16 generators / ≤ 8 outputs, absolute/`..`/symlink-escape paths and duplicate names rejected), verifies every declared output exists on exit 0, stages outputs as pipeline-generated files; absent/empty manifest = documented zero-behavior-change no-op; generator failure = loud `[generator:<name>]` FAIL (exit code + last 20 lines of combined output secret-scrubbed — key/token/secret/password/credential value assignments elided), no partial staging, later generators never run; the generator's --check form REMAINS the audit's drift gate (audit-variant.ts) — this step regenerates, it does not weaken any gate. Unit-pinned by tests/unit/generator-manifest.test.ts. Prior: v1.36.0 (2026-10-31, T-20261031-003 — spec docs/designs/2026-10-31-changelog-normalize-design.md §4): step 3b fail-then-heal — detector findings first trigger `bun scripts/changelog-normalize.ts CHANGELOG.md` (write mode; standalone script per design D1), then the file is re-staged and the detector re-run, asserting zero findings before the gate verdict; findings surviving normalization are a hard FAIL with the raw findings; zero findings never touch the file (no write, no diff). Migration path for legacy project CHANGELOG structures — the 2026-10-31 wave run had 6 of 11 projects blocked on the v1.35.0 fail-only gate (malformed dated `## [Unreleased] YYYY-MM-DD` variants plus duplicate category blocks; co-deck/co-newbiz multi-heading shapes) and the wave halted; the normalizer is structure-only and idempotent, so the write happens at most once per legacy structure, and `lang:` / `lang_reason:` frontmatter stays byte-identical. v1.35.0 (2026-10-29, U-20261028-013 — spec docs/designs/2026-10-29-delivery-bookkeeping-engine-design.md §3.A4): new step 3b — CHANGELOG structural-duplicate gate, blocking (step-3 precedent): duplicate `### <Category>` headings within one release section, or more than one `[Unreleased]` section (`^## [Unreleased]` prefix match — the live case is co-work carrying both `## [Unreleased]` and `## [Unreleased] 2026-09-01`), FAIL with a fix message naming the merge action; scan covers ALL `## [...]` sections; detection in helpers/changelog-duplicates.ts v1.0.0 (pure, unit-pinned by tests/unit/changelog-duplicate-gate.test.ts). v1.34.0 (2026-10-28, spec docs/designs/2026-10-28-platform-agent-mirror-design.md, DEC-20261028-06): new step 4.61 — agent-mirror parity check running `bun scripts/sync-agent-mirrors.ts --check` immediately after the step 4.6 skill sync. Blocking from day one (Q3 default): at the workspace root the step is a green no-op (mirrors are project-scoped; root dispatch reads agents/ directly), in delivered projects a drift blocks /sync until one deliberate write-mode run repairs it — /sync never silently repairs. | `hub-bootstrap.ts` | L0 | 1.6.0 | active | v1.6.0 (2026-11-02, Phase 4 remediation follow-up — sanctioned-scrub tolerance): the drill's SECOND false-positive class is cleared — the hook-integrity fingerprint normalizes BOTH sides through the sanctioned CONSTITUTION.md→context.md comment scrub (shared lib/hook-integrity.ts, single derivation with hub-probe's hooks_integrity signal) before hashing: the L1 delivery source (templates/common/scripts/hooks/) ships comment-scrubbed copies per §7.5 Non-Propagation, so the raw byte diff between root canonical and ANY correctly-delivered clone reported "hook differs from canonical" forever (the drill's gateguard-fact-force.ts "one-comment diff" survives the v1.5.0 wave unchanged). The code-file scrub rules touch comments only — functional changes (string literals, code, the @version header) still fail byte-exactly, and the scrub is idempotent. The v1.5.0/v1.6.0 comparison logic (incl. the /^_test-.*\.ts$/ exclusion) moved into lib/hook-integrity.ts v1.0.0 so the gate and the probe can never disagree. Paired test update (tests/unit/hub-bootstrap.test.ts v1.5.0): a scrub-only divergence PASSES the gate; the same delivered copy with an additionally-changed string literal still FAILS. Prior: v1.5.0 (2026-11-02, Phase 4 remediation — REAL lock leak + drill checker false-positive): (a) LOCK-LEAK FIX — fail()/failWithCleanup() called process.exit() directly, and process.exit terminates the VM without running intervening finally blocks, so the bootstrap automation lock (released in bootstrap()'s finally) leaked on EVERY gate-failure path — the 2026-11-02 fleet drill left .pipeline-state/automation-bootstrap-co-security.lock behind after a hook-integrity failure. fail() now throws a typed ExitSignal (exported) that unwinds through bootstrap()'s try/finally — the lock is released BEFORE the process exits — and the outermost CLI frame prints the ❌ verdict and converts it to the exit code exactly once; all fail() sites keep their messages and exit codes (only behavior change for library consumers: moveIntoPlace's same-volume failure now throws ExitSignal instead of killing the process). (b) _test-* EXCLUSION — scripts/hooks/_test-*.ts are root-only test helper modules (SCRIPTS.md registers them as internal test fixtures; no @version header — lifecycle Check A already treats them as version-unvalidatable), NOT delivered artifacts, so the hook-integrity comparison excludes /^_test-.*\.ts$/ from BOTH sides of the canonical diff — their root-only presence can no longer false-fail any hub (the drill failed co-security on _test-consumer.ts / _test-module.ts, which every clone legitimately lacks); shrinks the false-positive surface for every future hub. Paired test update (tests/unit/hub-bootstrap.test.ts v1.4.0): lock-leak regression (gate failure leaves NO .lock residue and the lock is immediately re-acquirable — verified to fail against v1.4.0), and the fixture's canonical hooks now carry the root-only _test-* fixtures while the seeded hub repo does not, making the E2E pass case the standing exclusion regression. Prior: v1.4.0 (2026-11-02, Windows CI fix round 4 — concurrent-claim errno): moveIntoPlace's concurrent-claim catch now includes EPERM alongside ENOTEMPTY/EEXIST — renaming a directory onto an EXISTING directory throws EPERM on Windows (libuv MoveFileExW) where POSIX reports ENOTEMPTY/EEXIST; same operation, platform errno difference (Windows CI failed the concurrent-claim test with "EPERM: operation not permitted, rename '...stage' -> '...Projects\co-x'"). The cleanup + typed-notice behavior is identical for all three codes and the "winner stands" outcome is unchanged; the EPERM branch is unreachable on POSIX for this rename shape, so no legitimate failure is masked. Sibling-rename audit: the script's only rename is moveIntoPlace's — the bootstrap-state.json marker is a direct writeFileSync onto a fresh path inside the temp clone (no rename, no hazard); helpers/hub-state.ts's writeStateAtomic renames FILE→FILE, and MoveFileExW with MOVEFILE_REPLACE_EXISTING replaces an existing regular file on Windows, so the EPERM-on-existing-target hazard is the dir→dir shape only — no further guards needed. Prior: v1.3.0 (2026-11-02, Windows portability hardening round 2 — CI round 2 failed the DRY-RUN path itself with a blind exit 1, so the early path is made hazard-free and every external spawn diagnosable): (a) run() spawns with windowsHide:true and, when a child cannot be spawned at all (status null + proc.error, e.g. ENOENT for a missing binary), surfaces the spawn error inside the returned output — a missing tool can no longer masquerade as a silent empty-output failure; (b) the gate's bun invocations use process.execPath (the bun binary already running the script) instead of re-resolving the bare name 'bun' from the ambient PATH — shim-dependent on Windows CI, and the same bare-name resolution was proven to fail with exactly the blind exit-1 signature when PATH lacks the binary; (c) gitleaks is existence-gated via Bun.which — a missing scanner is a typed fail-closed message naming the HUB_BOOTSTRAP_SKIP_EXTERNAL_CHECKS seam, never a misread "exit 1 = findings" verdict (spawn ENOENT also reads status null → 1); (d) moveIntoPlace's same-volume check compares only FINITE statSync().dev numbers — where a Windows/Bun runtime leaves dev NaN the check is skipped with a note instead of false-failing the atomic rename (documented platform guard; shell probes like `stat -f` stay forbidden); (e) resolvePinnedSha splits ls-remote output on /\r?\n/. Paired test update (tests/unit/hub-bootstrap.test.ts v1.2.0): self-describing expectExit/expectSilentStderr assertions (exit code + stderr + stdout head thrown on mismatch, original substring assertions kept), deadPid() via process.execPath (spawnSync('true') has no Windows binary and its -1 fallback reads EPERM=alive on POSIX), CLI runners spawn process.execPath with windowsHide:true, fixture git resolved once via Bun.which (typed throw when absent) and work-tree cleanup best-effort-retried for the Windows EBUSY handle race. Prior: v1.2.0 (2026-11-02, Windows CI fix — tests/unit/hub-bootstrap.test.ts 'hook-integrity divergence FAILS the gate'): the hook-integrity fingerprint is line-ending-neutral — sha256 over content with CRLF normalized to LF, posix-style walk keys — and the bootstrap's clone pins -c core.autocrlf=false, so a Windows autocrlf=true checkout can neither false-fail the pass case nor mask a real divergence (normalization is a no-op on POSIX; §4.4 byte-identity now reads byte-identity-modulo-line-endings); every cleanup-then-fail site routes through failWithCleanup/cleanupTemp (best-effort rmSync that never throws) — on Windows a throwing rmSync (EBUSY/EPERM while AV or a lingering git child holds a handle on a freshly cloned file) used to replace the typed VALIDATED gate verdict with an opaque cleanup exception (exit 1 without the typed message). Prior: v1.1.0 (2026-11-02, spec docs/designs/2026-11-01-hub-consolidation-design.md §3.5/§4.3.5/§3.4, Phase 3.5 audit completion): (a) §3.5 divergence force — while a hub's LATEST root-side probe report reads DIVERGENT and is newer than its last journal entry, `declare … AVAILABLE` is auto-forced to DEGRADED (the journal reason names the forcing report; a MATCH probe re-opens AVAILABLE) — report verdicts are parsed through hub-probe.ts's exported probeReportFiles/probeReportVerdict/latestProbeReportFile (single derivation of the report format); (b) §4.3.5 boot-time sweep — every --apply bootstrap sweeps dead-pid/>24h temp dirs BEFORE creating its own (sweepStaleTempDirs detection shared with --recover); dry-run never sweeps; (c) declare DEGRADED requires --capabilities when the existing degraded set is empty (§3.4 — matches the operator guide §5.3; re-attest reuse and the machine-coerced force are exempt). Prior: v1.0.0 (2026-11-02, spec docs/designs/2026-11-01-hub-consolidation-design.md §4 + §3.1, Phase 3.5): hub auto-creation — two cases one entry point: remote-exists = git clone from the registry hub_remote ONLY (hubs[] row by name, else the unique entries[] hub_remote); remote-missing = typed halt routing to the USER decision + ensure-github-repo.ts or scaffold (never creates a remote from this path). Pinned approval (§4.2): the plan prints hub + resolved SHA (git ls-remote main→HEAD) and REQUIRES --yes — the flag IS the recorded approval, journaled as the state file's approval record {actor: user, remote, sha, at}. Five guarantees (§4.5): idempotency (existing valid clone short-circuits; existing INVALID target is a typed refusal — never deletes or overwrites), lock (automation-lock.ts bootstrap-<hub> verbatim, released in finally), atomicity (temp dir Projects/.bootstrap-<hub>-<pid>-<ts> — the gw- namespace is forbidden because sweepOrphanedScaffolds would destroy it mid-run; same-volume dev check then atomic rename; EEXIST/ENOTEMPTY = concurrent claim — the loser cleans its temp and reports, not an error), recovery (bootstrap-state.json {pid,url,sha,started_at} marker + boot-time sweep of dead-pid and >24h temps on every --apply run, plus the manual --recover sweep, dry-run default), audit (BOOTSTRAPPED→VALIDATED→READY journal entries, actor hub-bootstrap, hash-chained via helpers/hub-state.ts; operational_state intentionally left null — the hub PM declares it). VALIDATED gate (§4.4, hooks stay inactive until it passes — the move happens only after all checks): gitleaks detect --config .gitleaks.toml (working tree, any finding fails), hook-integrity byte diff vs canonical scripts/hooks/ (always real), bun install + bun audit in the clone (any advisory fails — fail-closed beyond the no-high/critical floor), root audit.ts green. Also: status <hub> renderer, and declare <hub> <AVAILABLE,DEGRADED,UNAVAILABLE,RECOVERY> --actor <user,hub-pm> --reason ... [--capabilities a,b] [--lease-hours N] [--state-dir <dir>] — the hub PM's operational-state declaration duty (§3.2/§3.3; bootstrap states are machine-only and refused here; the §3.5 divergence force and the §3.4 --capabilities requirement apply — see v1.1.0). Test seams (documented): HUB_BOOTSTRAP_ROOT (workspace root redirect), HUB_BOOTSTRAP_SKIP_EXTERNAL_CHECKS=1 (skips the three external checks; recorded in the VALIDATED journal reason so a skipped validation is never silent), HUB_BOOTSTRAP_NOW. @l2-propagate: false. | `hub-probe.ts` | L0 | 1.3.0 | active | v1.3.0 (2026-11-03, CI determinism fix — deterministic clock seam): the probe now consumes the documented HUB_BOOTSTRAP_NOW seam (same contract as hub-bootstrap.ts nowIso()) — when set, TTL-lease expiry is evaluated at that absolute instant (Date.parse, ms, NaN-guarded fallback to Date.now()), not at the runner's wall clock. Before, probeHub judged the fixture's pinned lease (expires 2026-11-02T20:00Z) against the real clock, so every run after that instant (CI ubuntu first; the reported "TZ" symptom was actually run-time-of-day) read the live fixture lease as EXPIRED and flipped 4 MATCH-expected probe tests to DIVERGENT (exit 2). Lease math and report dating were already TZ-safe (absolute ms; date = KST via kst-time.ts) — no host-local time anywhere, no platform conditionals. Paired test update (tests/unit/hub-bootstrap.test.ts v1.6.0): dual-TZ regression runs the same fixture under child TZ=UTC and TZ=Asia/Seoul and asserts byte-identical reports + identical MATCH verdicts, so any host-local calendar parse (naive Date string, toLocaleDateString, local-midnight lease math) reintroduced into the probe path fails loudly instead of only on CI. Prior: v1.2.0 (2026-11-02, Phase 4 remediation follow-up — drill false-positive classes): the hooks_integrity quick-check compares through the SHARED lib/hook-integrity.ts normalization (single derivation with hub-bootstrap's VALIDATED gate) instead of a raw byte diff — (a) the root-only _test-*.ts test fixtures are excluded from BOTH sides (never delivered; their root-only presence read as divergence on every hub probe), (b) the sanctioned CONSTITUTION.md→context.md comment scrub (§7.5 Non-Propagation) no longer reads as divergence (the drill's gateguard-fact-force.ts "one-comment diff"). Real divergence — code, string literals, a missing or extra hook — still reports MISMATCH; the "match (N files)" count is over the delivered files after the fixture exclusion. Left unfixed, §3.5 would have auto-forced every AVAILABLE declaration to DEGRADED on these non-divergences. Paired test: hub-probe MATCH despite _test-* + scrub-only divergence; a functional hook change still reports MISMATCH. Prior: v1.1.0 (2026-11-02, spec docs/designs/2026-11-01-hub-consolidation-design.md §3.5, Phase 3.5 audit completion): the report-format knowledge exported for single-derivation reuse — probeReportFiles / latestProbeReportFile / probeReportVerdict consumed by hub-bootstrap.ts's declaration-side divergence force; no behavior change to the probe itself. Prior: v1.0.0 (2026-11-02, spec docs/designs/2026-11-01-hub-consolidation-design.md §3.5, Phase 3.5/3-common): ROOT-SIDE READ-ONLY hub probe — bun scripts/hub-probe.ts [--all or <hub>...] reads each hub's local state file plus observable signals (repo present, variant.json and template-version.txt, hook-dir byte-integrity vs canonical scripts/hooks/, ticket-store writability via access(W_OK) — no writes into the hub, ever) and records observed vs declared values into docs/reports/YYYY-MM-DD-hub-probe-<hub>.md with a MATCH or DIVERGENT verdict and keep-latest-4 retention per hub (date = KST). Divergence rules (§3.5): broken hash chain (history rewriting is forbidden), a declared operational state standing on an EXPIRED TTL lease (consumers read UNAVAILABLE), and the chained-probe rule (the prior probe report's head_hash must still be present in the current history — append-only proof; a missing head means the journal was rewritten between probes). DIVERGENT prints the SEV ticket command (SEV2 → P1 per hub-kanban-aliases.json) for the operator or lifecycle-manager to file — the probe NEVER auto-files; exit code 2 lets the weekly check branch. Weekly cadence is manual (no cron wired). Test seam: HUB_PROBE_ROOT (falls back to HUB_BOOTSTRAP_ROOT). @l2-propagate: false. Tests: tests/unit/hub-bootstrap.test.ts | —| L0 | —|

| `helpers/hub-state.ts` | L0 | 1.0.0 | active | v1.0.0 (2026-11-02, spec docs/designs/2026-11-01-hub-consolidation-design.md §3, Phase 3.5): hub lifecycle state file — read/write/transition the hub-local .pipeline-state/hub-<hub>.state.json (atomic tmp+rename, same pattern as ticket-store.ts). Schema: hub, bootstrap_state, operational_state, degraded_capabilities[], ttl_lease, state_history[], head_hash, approval. state_history entries are hash-CHAINED (entry.hash = sha256(canonicalJson(entry minus hash) + prev_hash); canonicalJson sorts keys recursively) — validateChain detects any tamper, truncation, or head drift; history rewriting is forbidden (§3.5). applyTransition enforces the two-enum machine: bootstrap_states linear forward-only (null→BOOTSTRAPPED→VALIDATED→READY; machine-declared by hub-bootstrap.ts only — a hub-pm actor is refused); operational edges null→AVAILABLE or DEGRADED or UNAVAILABLE, AVAILABLE↔DEGRADED, AVAILABLE/DEGRADED→UNAVAILABLE, UNAVAILABLE→RECOVERY→AVAILABLE or UNAVAILABLE, same-state re-attest; illegal edges throw HubStateError. TTL leases (§3.3, default 8h): isLeaseValid + effectiveOperationalState (an expired declaration reads UNAVAILABLE; null operational_state = not yet in service). DEGRADED capability matrix (§3.4): validateDegradedCapabilities refuses unknown capability_enum keys, never_in_degraded capabilities, and capabilities the hub's hubs[] row never declared (fail-closed when no row exists). Registry enum cross-check via resolveRegistryEnumsForHub/loadRegistryEnums (helpers/hub-topology.ts loadHubTopology). Consumers: scripts/hub-bootstrap.ts (init/journal/declare), scripts/hub-probe.ts (read-only), scripts/ticket.ts (UNAVAILABLE gate). @l2-propagate: false. Tests: tests/unit/hub-state.test.ts | —| L0 | —|

Prior: v1.2.0 (2026-11-04, U-20261104-002): workspace-root detection — the constructor pinned the workspace boundary to process.cwd(), so a tool running inside a variant PROJECT (cwd = the project root) classified the documented cross-tier extends contract (extends: ../../../agents/pm.md — the 3-tier upgrade contract at agents/pm.md:2 / agents/docs-writer.md:2) as a path-traversal escape and failed closed. The boundary is now detected by walking upward from cwd for an L0-only workspace marker (the L0-only governance SSOT file or templates/common — both verified absent from every delivered Projects/co-X checkout), exported as detectWorkspaceRoot() and unit-pinned by tests/unit/security-validator-workspace-root.test.ts. Precedence unchanged and fail-closed: explicit options.workspace_root always wins; no marker found = fallback to cwd (never wider than the evidence); traversal escaping the detected root still fails; boundary comparison is symlink-canonicalized (macOS /var to /private/var) and separator-aware (a sibling directory sharing the root name prefix cannot pass). | `helpers/memory-union-verify.ts` | L0+L1 | 1.7.0 | active | v1.7.0 (2026-11-23, T-20261123-009 — spec docs/designs/2026-11-23-fleet-graph-machinery-batch-design.md §2): the step-2 sparing inventory learns the merge-delivered class — owedBlocks() now also emits a `merged-present` entry per block of each scanned merge's MERGED blob (the blob was already fetched for droppedBlocks and previously discarded; now parsed — no new git plumbing). The PR #2160 incident: a merge DELIVERED a theirs-side branch=main session block into the merged blob, a non-merge loss removed it from the committed state, and the manual restore was re-collapsed by a retry's step-2 same-branch replace because the inventory keyed on MERGE-TIME debt only — a block present in `merged` produces no droppedBlocks entry, so the restored segment had nothing to fingerprint-match. Fix = git-side MEMBERSHIP, deliberately NO run-time worktree delta (the restored block IS in the worktree at the fateful run, so "merged blob minus current worktree" is empty exactly when sparing must fire — the same paradox v1.5.0 solved for merge-time debt). New OwedBlock.source discriminator: "merge-drop" (existing class, entries unchanged) / "merged-present"; `parent` on merged-present entries is INFORMATIONAL-ONLY ("ours" when the ours parent carries the block under the matchesHead fingerprint rules, "theirs" otherwise); classes disjoint by construction — no dedup, sparing is a boolean some(). Worktree gating unchanged (absent worktree file / merged===null deleted-file arm contribute nothing). dev-sync step 2's isOwedSegment consumes the extended inventory UNCHANGED (generic fingerprint matching) — dev-sync stays 1.53.0. Consequence: restoring a merge-delivered block WITH its `branch=` marker now survives step-2 replace/collapse; the marker-less workaround is no longer required (landed marker-less legacy blocks append/never-match, unchanged). Accepted over-breadth (fail-safe, self-healing — the v1.5.0 consequence class): a same-branch segment matching a merged-present block is spared even when the block is also in HEAD/worktree until the merge ages out of the 7-day/20-merge window. Pinned by tests/unit/memory-union-verify.test.ts MP-T1..T4 (MP-T1 demonstrated RED at 1.6.0 / GREEN at 1.7.0; OW-T1..T4 and the v1.6.0 loop pin stay green unchanged). Prior: v1.6.0 (2026-11-20, U-20261120-001 — spec docs/designs/2026-11-20-runner-hardening-integrity-design.md §3.5, runner-hardening PR-4): the exit-128 noise closure — `git()` gains an `absentOk` option: exit 128 from `git show <commit>:<path>` is the DOCUMENTED absent-path signal (file added/deleted relative to that side), so showBlob (the ONLY absentOk caller) collects the ref into a module-level collector and returns null SILENTLY instead of the anonymous incomplete-sweep WARN that made every merge-touched one-side-taken file emit up to 3 WARN rows per sweep (20+ per 14-day scan in Projects/co-security). `unionScan` and `owedBlocks` each end with ONE INFO line naming the collected refs (`N absent-side blob reads (documented no-data — file added/deleted between sides): ref1, ref2, ref3 [+k more]`, capped at 3 named + [+k more]), then drain — two INFO lines per dev-sync pipeline worst case, each naming refs, vs 20+ anonymous WARNs. The strict WARN (every non-showBlob call — log, diff-tree, show -s — keeps the strict channel) now also NAMES its failing ref (`git <cmd> failed (exit <code>) on <args[1] ?? args.join(" ")>`) so genuine failures stop being anonymous. Design item 2's justification: all showBlob SHAs come from the same repo's `git log --merges` moments earlier, so an unknown-SHA 128 is not a real shape here; corrupt-object pathologies surface through the remaining strict calls. The earlier same-id report (restore-then-re-collapse loop) is regression-pinned, not re-fixed — v1.5.0 spare semantics cover it by design; the new test drives the full convergence chain (merge-time debt inventoried for step-2 sparing + gate tiers the restored worktree repaired-at-head + both hold on the same-branch re-run). Pinned by tests/unit/memory-union-verify.test.ts (a: no WARN storm; b: exactly one INFO naming `<parent-sha>:<file>`; c: strict WARN in a non-repo names `--merges`; d: the loop pin). Prior: v1.5.0 (2026-11-18, T-20261118-003 — spec docs/designs/2026-11-18-memory-gate-step2-deadlock-design.md §3.1): new exported owedBlocks() — the gate's debt inventory as a pure composition of listMemoryMerges + showBlob + droppedBlocks; consumed by dev-sync 1.52.0 step 2 to spare same-branch segments the union gate still owes, closing the T-20261118-003 restore-then-re-collapse FATAL loop on branch=main sessions. Head-side clarification vs §3.1 prose (verified during implementation by DL-T1 red/green): the inventory is the MERGE-TIME debt the Arm A findings flag — droppedBlocks against the MERGED BLOB (the same call verifyUnion tiers on, §0's "a parent-owed block per droppedBlocks") — because debt against the current worktree zeroes out exactly when a restoration is present (the restored block satisfies the debt before step 2 runs), making the sparing unfireable in the deadlock it exists to close; the worktree still gates the inventory per merge-touched file (absent file → contributes nothing — the tier arms own that state; merged===null deleted-file arm likewise skipped). Tier-agnostic by design: deliberately no activationIso parameter (keeps step 2 free of the GATE_3_88_ACTIVATED_AT TDZ — declared after step 2 in dev-sync). Accepted consequence (design R4-class): a block whose repair is already committed stays in the inventory until the merge ages out — a same-branch verbatim re-match is spared (content preserved, self-healing). Pinned by tests/unit/memory-union-verify.test.ts (OW-T1..T4). Prior: v1.4.0 (2026-11-18, T-20261117-008 — spec docs/designs/2026-11-18-memory-union-archival-tier-design.md §2): the archival tier now also satisfies block-drop findings for merges whose dropped-block file was LATER archived — the T-20261117-008 shape (merge 59ad0495695f existed-at-merge drop, housekeeping c5c57d10b archived both logs; 3 live /sync FATALs on 2026-11-17 co-deck PR-2/PR-4/PR-5; every fresh worktree re-FATALs until the merge ages out of the 7-day window). When neither the worktree file nor the gitignored memory/archive/<basename> copy is readable, unionScan consults the MEMORY.md tombstone row for the file (tracked; same regex as the v1.1.1 merged===null arm, extracted as hasMemoryTombstone and reused by both arms) — tombstone present = sanctioned archival provenance = tier 'historical' (WARN, ages out with the window; drop content stays recoverable from git history); no tombstone keeps the original tier (fail-closed). An archive copy that IS readable but does not carry the parent-union stays blocking (readable evidence outranks provenance, locally actionable). Fresh clones get the WARN, not the FATAL — the documented degradation (machinery design docs/designs/2026-11-17-root-machinery-batch-design.md §6). Pinned by tests/unit/memory-union-verify.test.ts (T-20261117-008 fixture: historical / no-tombstone blocking / damaged-archive blocking / parent-union repaired-at-head). Out of scope: what counts as a drop (unchanged), the runner stop-file semantics ruling (separate), dev-sync step-3.88 historical-line wording (follow-up). Prior: v1.3.0 (2026-11-17, U-20261116-012 PR-2 compat — design docs/designs/2026-11-17-root-machinery-batch-design.md §2.2 item 4): the block-start well-formedness rule accepts a leading single-line HTML comment before the first '## ' heading — dev-sync 1.50.0 prefixes its session blocks with a `<!-- dev-sync:session branch=<branch> -->` identity marker (one block per branch per day) and this gate's Arm B runs FATAL on every flat memory log, so without the relaxation every post-1.50.0 memory log would fail "block does not begin with a '## ' heading". Scope deliberately narrow: only whole single-line `<!-- ... -->` lines at the top of a block are skipped; prose, unterminated comments, and multi-line comments still fail, and the block/separator count invariant plus the fused-heading check are untouched. Pinned by tests/unit/dev-sync-rerun-hygiene.test.ts (B-T8). Prior: v1.2.0 (2026-11-06, T-20261106-007 — the gate-deadlock closure): the archive vs union-verify fight — merge e73b51057 deleted memory/2026-10-29.md (archive-memory retention, post-activation); the gate's prescribed repair (restore in the working tree) was immediately undone by step 3.8's archive move (memory/archive/), and with the file absent the repaired-at-head check could not run — permanently FATAL. Fix: the repaired-at-head check falls back to memory/archive/<basename> when the worktree file is absent — the archive is the documented end-state and its content IS the parent blocks (relocated, not destroyed). Also (T-20261106-005 item 2): git plumbing failures now WARN to stderr (an incomplete sweep is observable, no longer indistinguishable from an empty window). Prior: v1.1.1 (2026-11-06, T-20261105-002 follow-through — first post-merge sync finding): the deleted-file arm (archive-memory 7-day retention deletes a flat log; merge e73b51057 landed that deletion post-activation) pushed an unconditional FATAL "file deleted while a parent still carries session blocks" — archival deletions are the documented retention cycle, now tiered repaired-at-head (INFO) when the MEMORY.md index row for the deleted log survives (the tombstone is the positive evidence, the same positive-evidence-only posture as filterTransientCheckouts); no tombstone keeps the original tier (a genuine clobber deletion stays FATAL). Unit-pinned by the archival-deletion fixture. Prior: v1.1.0 (2026-11-05, T-20261105-002): the v1 gap closed — post-activation drop findings were unrecoverable because Arm A compared the merge commit's OWN blob, so "restore in the working tree — the repair lands forward" could never clear a finding (design §5.3's "a repaired file passes" premise did not hold; live-blocked since merge c8aba5d61). New: droppedBlocks (parent-labeled strict-missing extraction, verifyUnion refactored onto it) + repairedAtHead (every parent-owed block present in the working-tree content under the strict rules PLUS one absorption tolerance — an UN-HEADED parent block counts as restored when its trimmed text appears verbatim in the head content; headed blocks keep the strict heading+firstLine rule so containment cannot fake a heading) + unionScan re-tiers such groups 'repaired-at-head' (INFO) by reading the worktree file; unreadable worktree keeps the original tier. Consumed by dev-sync v1.41.0 step 3.88. Prior: v1.0.0 (2026-11-05, T-20261104-001 — spec docs/designs/2026-11-05-merge-integrity-design.md §5.1): memory session-block union-verify — the gate for git ort auto-merge / conflict-resolution corruption of append-only memory/YYYY-MM-DD.md session logs (2026-11-04 incidents: dropped blocks + fused heading/separator, no conflict markers, hand-repaired). Pure exported core: parseBlocks (verify-memory.ts:109 split convention), verifyUnion (every block owed by either parent must appear in the merged result — exact trimmed fingerprint first, heading+firstLine fallback for cosmetic reflows, order/dedup-tolerant; plus fuse detection: any block with >= 2 '## Session Summary' headings — and the block/separator count invariant) and verifyWellFormed (Arm B structural integrity). Thin git plumbing: listMemoryMerges (bounded 7-day/20-merge sweep, --full-history required — default history simplification prunes TREESAME-to-one-parent merges, the clean-union shape this gate must bless), showBlob, unionScan (two-tier: activationIso cutoff — post-activation merges blocking, pre-activation historical, fail-closed on undatable commits), wellFormedScan. Exemptions mirror verify-memory.ts:37-42 + memory/archive/. Consumed by dev-sync step 3.88 (BLOCKING, two-tier rollout — see the dev-sync v1.40.0 row). Shipped to templates/common/scripts/helpers byte-identical (imported by the L1 dev-sync mirror); fully armed in delivered trees — a variant repo with no memory merge commits simply passes | —| L0+L1 | —|

Rationale, verified on Bun 1.4.4: `$.env(obj)` REPLACES the whole child environment — `.env({GIT_DIR: undefined})` + `printenv PATH` prints nothing and exits 1, while `.env(process.env)` prints the full PATH — so an `{key: undefined}` map is the ENTIRE child env (incident chain: rogue commits → unset-map 'fix' → missing PATH → 26 of 268 unit files failing at dev-sync step 4.85 with `Executable not found in $PATH: "git"`). gitCleanEnv() unchanged for spawnSync callers (spawnSync merges over process.env). v1.1.0 (2026-11-09, rogue-commit incident): added gitEnvUnset() (RETRACTED in v1.2.0), used at every pipeline test spawn and by the git-aware helpers; layer raised to L0+L1 because the delivered hooks (pre-push) and the delivered helpers now import it. v1.0.0 (2026-11-09, spec docs/designs/2026-11-08-gw-tenant-loop-and-hardening-design.md): git repo-redirection environment sanitizer — gitCleanEnv(base?) copies base (or process.env) minus the nine redirection vars (GIT_DIR, GIT_WORK_TREE, GIT_INDEX_FILE, GIT_OBJECT_DIRECTORY, GIT_ALTERNATE_OBJECT_DIRECTORIES, GIT_COMMON_DIR, GIT_CEILING_DIRECTORIES, GIT_NAMESPACE, GIT_CONFIG; identity/config vars like GIT_AUTHOR_* and GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL are kept; GIT_REDIRECT_ENV_KEYS is exported for validators). Pure, idempotent (second run inserts nothing, byte-identical). Positions planned in original coordinates, applied back-to-front. Span-local correction, NOT restamp-classified (restamps[] semantics untouched; the swap never changes a line count); idempotent (a re-run over merged content is a silent no-op — `merged: false`, no RESTAMPED line — because its only remaining difference is the date line the guard restores); count-mismatch reconcile stays out of scope (already WARNING + snapshot + recovery file). Prior: v1.35.0 (2026-11-01, spec docs/designs/2026-11-01-hub-consolidation-design.md Phase 1 batch C): PLACEHOLDER_ALLOWLIST gains the 5 co-security assurance-suite canonical SKILL.md paths (skills/{samm-maturity,sarif-exporter,spdx-sbom,stride-threat-matrix,finding-reconciliation}/SKILL.md) — their {{PROJECT_*}} tokens are hub-doctrine derivation parameters (each file's Co-develop Integration Note defines unfilled defaults + spoke bindings), substituted when a spoke variant adopts the doctrine, never by scaffolding; the strict check-upgrade-coverage placeholder gate must not flag them. Skill-level keys cover the platform mirrors via the checker's mirror-prefix strip. Prior: v1.34.0 (2026-11-01, design addendum docs/designs/2026-10-31-workspace-schema-merge-delivery-design.md §Addendum, U-20261101-001 + T-20261101-001): SCHEMA_TEMPLATE_AUTHORITATIVE_KEYS = [models, platform_surfaces] — the template-authoritative vocabulary blocks land the template value VERBATIM with TEMPLATE_DELTA INFO divergence rows (the 0.26.0 wave MERGE-pass-stamps-prose/SCHEMA_MERGE-withholds-rows desync failed validate-model-registry exact-equality on all 15 wave projects; the manual 3-value convergence is now unnecessary); DELETE verdict joins DecisionVerdict + DECISION_SEVERITY_CONTRACT at WARN (T-20261101-001 adjudication — registry-curated prune deletions are never silent). Prior: v1.33.0 (2026-10-31, design docs/designs/2026-10-31-graft-trail-migration-design.md R2.4 arm (b), U-20261031-009): TRAIL_RETIRED_SEEDS + isTrailRetiredSeed(rel) — the graft-era seeds (.mcp.json, .claude/helpers/graft-hooks.cjs, .claude/helpers/graft-statusline.cjs) a `.trail/`-marked project treats as retired; consulted by the upgrade TEMPLATE TREE SYNC pass before every absent-copy delivery (SEED_SKIP retired-surface), no resolver/classifier change. Prior: v1.32.0 (2026-10-31, spec docs/designs/2026-10-31-workspace-schema-merge-delivery-design.md): merge-aware delivery for docs/workspace-schema.json — the co-price 0.22.1→0.25.0 / co-architect 0.23.0→0.25.0 upgrade halts. The `SCHEMA_MERGE` policy joins UpgradePolicy + isDeliveryPolicy, claimed by exactly docs/workspace-schema.json via SCHEMA_MERGE_FILE (resolveClaim, above the docs/ SYNC fall-through). mergeWorkspaceSchemaData(template, project) — the block-aware three-way top-level merge (design §4): the template-owned class (absent-from-project blocks + the $schema/version/description metadata scalars) lands verbatim; project-owned blocks are preserved untouched; shared blocks recurse leaf-wise with project-wins on existing leaves, template-only keys landing, and ARRAYS ATOMIC (never unioned — the deliberate divergence from mergeSettingsData's settings semantics, whose array-union would have appended the template's playground scanRoot to co-price's ["src"] and whose template-wins scalars would have clobbered project-evolved leaf values). Pure in (template, project) → idempotent (double merge is a fixed point, deep + byte equality). Returns the merged object plus landed/preserved top-level key lists and templateDeltas — every shared leaf whose final value differs from the template's (a withheld template change), each with a dotted path, summarized both-values, and severity (WARN for scalar/array divergences, INFO for structural mismatches) that the engine turns into TEMPLATE_DELTA decision rows. mergeWorkspaceSchemaJson — the file-level wrapper with the mergeSettingsJson output convention (2-space indent + trailing newline; key order follows the project copy, template-added keys append in template order). schemaDeepEqual is structural (key-order-only differences never read as false deltas). TEMPLATE_DELTA (INFO default, WARN via the per-row override) joins DecisionVerdict + DECISION_SEVERITY_CONTRACT. tests/unit/workspace-schema-merge.test.ts covers the design's 8 fixtures. Prior: v1.31.0 (2026-10-29, spec docs/designs/2026-10-29-delivery-bookkeeping-engine-design.md, DEC-20261029-01 — PR1): TOMBSTONE_CLEARED (INFO) and STUB_VERSION_DIVERGENCE (INFO) join DecisionVerdict + DECISION_SEVERITY_CONTRACT. TOMBSTONE_CLEARED records an applied --clear-tombstone grant that deleted ≥ 1 manifest entry (the retraction becomes auditable in the unified decision log instead of a silent manifest rewrite; schemaVersion stays 2 — additive verdicts only). STUB_VERSION_DIVERGENCE surfaces the stub-backfill contract's sanctioned divergence: an existing stub `version` differing from its template counterpart is a deliberate pin (INFO, never WARN; the backfill never overwrites it). Prior: v1.30.0 (2026-10-28, design docs/designs/2026-10-28-instruction-ownership-backport-hardening-design.md D1/D2): instruction-file region taxonomy + merge-time census — REGION_IN_SYNC (INFO) / EVOLVED_REGION (WARN) / TEMPLATE_AHEAD_REGION (WARN) join DecisionVerdict + DECISION_SEVERITY_CONTRACT; pure helpers INSTRUCTION_FILE_RELS, scanInstructionRegions (I1 managed zones + skeleton-registry sections / I2 VARIANT-* project-state marker sections / I3 free-form), censusInstructionRegions (out-of-zone LCS diff → per-block rows; zero rows for an in-sync file), spanCarriesProjectAuthoredLines (write-channel predicate; zone interiors stripped), InstructionCensusGate. Prior: v1.29.0 (2026-10-28, T-20261028-013 — spec docs/designs/2026-10-28-instruction-ownership-backport-hardening-design.md D9/Q5): STUB_VERSION_BUMP_ADVICE (INFO) joins DecisionVerdict + DECISION_SEVERITY_CONTRACT — the upgrade-project agents/ pass emits it when a stub-composed file reaches the equal-version drift arm with templateChanged (stub-owned fields moved template-side without the stub `version` bump that would have made the change arrive as a normal version-gated ADOPT); advisory-only this wave (Q5 default), the validate-templates enforcement check is a follow-up if drift appears. Prior: v1.28.0 (2026-10-28, T-20261028-010 — spec docs/designs/2026-10-28-stub-frontmatter-backfill-design.md): exports `backfillStubFrontmatter(projectContent, templateContent, deliveryDate)` — fills MISSING top-level scalar tier/version/last_updated keys in a project extends-stub from its template counterpart (tier from the counterpart's scalar comment-stripped, version verbatim, last_updated = the DELIVERY date not the counterpart's authoring date), and STUB_FM_BACKFILL (INFO) joins DecisionVerdict + DECISION_SEVERITY_CONTRACT. The validate-model-registry v2.1.0 missing-tier FAIL flip (template-v0.22.0) turned stubs whose frontmatter predates the field red, and no delivery pass adds a missing key: the v1.24.0 normalizer rewrites an EXISTING tier block only, and the agents/ version-gated passes replace frontmatter wholesale. Guards: the project content must itself be stub-shaped (a column-0 `extends:` in the frontmatter — resolved agent bodies are never touched, the v1.24.0 ownership contract stays intact); MISSING = the column-0 key is absent (existing values are never overwritten — deliberate project divergence stays; empty-value keys stay out of scope; nested lifecycle-block keys are never touched); a map-form or absent counterpart side fills nothing (never invent structure); filled keys append at the end of the frontmatter body; CRLF preserved; idempotent; body bytes untouched (frontmatter ownership for stubs is template-side, body stays project-owned, cf. U-20261028-001). Prior: v1.27.0 (2026-10-28, U-20261028-001 — spec docs/designs/2026-10-28-delivery-tombstone-ownership-design.md, D1–D3 + D6): delivery tombstones — additive data + one pure guard predicate, NO resolver/classifier change. TombstoneEntry + DeliveryManifestV2.tombstones (optional additive key, schemaVersion stays 2 — the v1→v2 grace pattern): { [rel]: { hash, templateVersion, recordedAt, reason } }, hash the last delivered baseline (baselineKeyFor form) at recording time. TOMBSTONED (ERROR — first observation of a project-side deletion of a previously delivered file; delivery refused until an explicit --tombstone <glob> confirms the intent) and TOMBSTONED_SKIP (INFO — the recorded deletion is honored; the path stays absent) join DecisionVerdict + DECISION_SEVERITY_CONTRACT. tombstoneGuard(rel, prior, absent) implements the D2 fire condition `absent(project, rel) && (prior.tombstones[rel] or prior.baselines[rel])`; consulted by every absent-copy delivery branch BEFORE the copy; never reads the disposition flag — per D3b --disposition adopt-template cannot override a tombstone (clearing requires --clear-tombstone). Firing only for template-shipped candidates makes template-side renames false-positive-free (design §4.5). Prior: v1.26.0 (2026-10-28, T-20261028-002 — spec docs/designs/2026-10-28-ticket-runner-mechanical-batch-design.md §3): globToRegExp zero-segment semantics — `/**/` also matches zero intermediate segments (a/**/b matches a/b) and a leading `**/` matches top level (**/b matches b); the old `.*`-substitution demanded literal slashes around the wildcard, so narrowed --disposition adopt-template globs silently skipped exactly those paths. Trailing `/**` and bare `**` unchanged. Prior: v1.25.0 (2026-10-27, T-20261027-004 — spec docs/designs/2026-10-27-sync-overwrite-policy-design.md, D1/D2/D3): delivery-decision observability data + pure helpers — NO classification change (the deny-list resolver untouched). New exports: `DeliveryDecision`/`DeliveryManifestV2` types (manifest v2 schema §7, `files` array preserved verbatim for isDeliveredDiff + PR-body compose), `DECISION_SEVERITY_CONTRACT` (INFO mechanics / WARN divergence / ERROR needs explicit disposition), `sha256Hex` + `baselineKeyFor` (D1 delivered-baseline provenance keys — raw bytes for plain files, lifecycleless bytes for agents/ so the post-delivery re-stamp and lifecycle-block preservation never read as project evolution), `classifyProjectState` (UNTOUCHED == baseline / IN_SYNC == today's deliverable / PROJECT_EVOLVED == both differ / NO_BASELINE = pre-baseline degrade per design §8), `resolveDisposition` (D2 matrix: project-evolved + template-changed under a version-gated overwrite pass → CONFLICT severity ERROR, default SKIP, explicit adopt-template disposition delivers; template-unchanged evolution delivers as EVOLVED WARN), `globToRegExp` (--disposition adopt-template[=glob] narrowing), `unresolvedErrorDecisions` + `manifestHasUnresolvedErrors` (dev-sync step 3.9 D3.1 gate). Prior: v1.24.0 (2026-10-27, T-20261027-005): exports `normalizeAgentTierFrontmatter()` — the ADR-0102 scalar-tier frontmatter normalizer for the upgrade MERGE (agents/pm.md) and agents/ delivery passes; replaces ONLY the legacy per-platform tier map with the template's scalar value when the template side is already scalar (template authoritative for the managed key), returns conflict:true for a deliberate scalar divergence, idempotent, CRLF-preserving, never invents structure (no tier key / empty tier key / map-form template → no-op). Prior: v1.23.0 (2026-10-27, U-20261027-002 + U-20261026-009): `scripts-snapshot.json` and `.claude/last-upgrade-pr-body.md` join PIPELINE_ARTIFACT_PATHS — both are regenerated by upgrade-project AFTER the delivery manifest is written, so they can never appear in manifest.files and their content change hard-blocked dev-sync step 3.9 on a purely delivered diff (2026-10-27 fleet upgrade wave); lifecyclelessText also strips top-level `last_updated:` lines so the agents/ drift compare tolerates the post-delivery re-stamp (bump-only date difference ≠ content drift, same tolerance as agent-lifecycle-audit's isMetadataOnlyChange). Prior: v1.22.0 (2026-10-07, U-20261006-001): `scripts-snapshot.json` moves from PROJECT_STATE_FILES to REGENERATED_FILES — generated state (scaffold/adopt writes it via helpers/write-scripts-snapshot.ts, upgrade-project now regenerates it post-upgrade), never template-delivered, never a frozen-at-scaffold artifact (PROJECT_STATE preservation made the Script version comparison re-report the same stale snapshot drift every upgrade). Owner derives in-repo from the workspace origin URL (no new config surface). Ruling (design §2): (a) remote exists AND no conforming origin → refuse, NO override flag (fleet-membership B1 refusal precedent, DEC-20261107-01); (b) remote exists AND origin already points at it → WARN + proceed (deliberate checkout shape — classification only, the pre-existing directory-exists refusal still dominates); (c) probe failure (gh missing/unauthenticated/offline) or owner-derivation failure → WARN/INFO + proceed — a network probe never blocks an offline scaffold. Primitives in lib/remote-collision.ts v1.0.0. | `sync-agent-mirrors.ts` | L0 | 1.4.0 | active | v1.4.0 (2026-11-28, U-20261128-003 — spec docs/designs/2026-11-28-sync-worktree-robustness-design.md §3): extends-pointer resolution goes through the shared canonical resolver (lib/extends-resolution.ts) — a `../`-anchored pointer strips the `../` run and anchors the remainder at the discovered workspace root, then falls back to the exact legacy file-dir-relative target, then the templates convention. The old math was depth-sensitive: from the sync-skill-documented worktree layout (Projects/<repo>-worktrees/<slug>) the pointer landed one ancestor short (Projects/agents/<name>.md, nonexistent), so pm.md's mirror went unverifiable (parity WARN via the L1-body fallback) and docs-writer.md FATALed the parity gate (UnresolvableStubError). Canonical and legacy math agree identically at depth 2 — canonical-layout mirrors are byte-identical (first post-fix --check is green with zero writes); the worktree layout re-resolves pm.md to the L0 body (parity restores without a write) and makes docs-writer.md verifiable again. Unresolvable errors name every searched candidate + ancestor. Prior: v1.3.1 (2026-11-22, U-20261121-002): EOL-insensitive mirror parity — the SSOT body is LF-normalized at read time and the on-disk mirror is normalized before comparison, so a CRLF working copy (Windows + core.autocrlf) no longer reports every agent as "differing" (the projection used to be mixed-EOL by construction: raw body + "\n"-joined frontmatter). Prior: v1.3.0 (2026-10-29, U-20261029-001 — T-20261029-008 follow-up): per-agent CHECK-mode degrade replaces the v1.2.0 blanket skip, which both concealed REAL drift among verifiable non-stub mirrors in a detached checkout and still hard-threw when no mirrors existed yet (the co-newbiz `git archive` CI repro). Stub bodies that cannot resolve now throw UnresolvableStubError; in --check that agent degrades — an EXISTING mirror is unverifiable (content not compared; the WARN + "run inside a workspace checkout or pass --templates-root" repair line are echoed on stdout so the audit arm logs them), a MISSING mirror is real drift (exit 1 — presence is always enforceable). Non-stub agents compare normally in every checkout. WRITE mode unchanged: hard error. Prior: v1.2.0 v1.2.0 (2026-10-28, T-20261029-008): CHECK-mode out-of-root degrade — a standalone CI checkout has no workspace ancestor, so extends-stub discovery hard-threw inside the SSOT parse and the audit gate blocked every real-CI project PR (co-abap #227, co-safety #220); when no templates root is discoverable AND generated mirrors exist, --check returns green with an explicit WARN (parity cannot be verified out-of-root — run inside a workspace checkout or pass --templates-root), write mode keeps the hard error (deliberate write, never auto-repair); discovery extracted to the exported pure discoverTemplatesRoot({root, searched}) reused by resolveTemplatesRoot (same bounded walk, same error text). Prior: v1.1.0 (2026-10-28, canary finding on Projects/co-newbiz — design §4.2 flagged ASSUMPTION verified with a real gap): extends-stub L1 bodies are sourced via templates-root DISCOVERY — the first ancestor of the target root carrying templates/common/agents (bounded 8-level walk), with an explicit `--templates-root <workspace>/templates` override (validated against the common/agents shape before any per-file work) taking precedence; not-found is a hard error naming every searched ancestor, never a silent fallback. In-project runs previously resolved the stub source against <project>/templates, which never exists in a delivered project. Mirror OUTPUT bytes unchanged — only where the stub body is sourced from; new-project/adopt-project wiring passes the workspace templates/ explicitly. Prior: v1.0.0 v1.0.0 (2026-10-28, spec docs/designs/2026-10-28-platform-agent-mirror-design.md, DEC-20261028-06, PR-1 root scope): initial — one template-managed generator projecting the platform-neutral agents/*.md SSOT (scalar `tier:` only, NO `model:` field) into the mirror directory of every registry-marked agents-capable platform (docs/workspace-schema.json platform_surfaces; v1 = Claude Code only). Idempotent compare-then-write with atomic writes, provenance manifest (.sync-agent-mirrors-managed.json) so user-added mirrors are never removed and no/corrupt manifest removes nothing, PID-stamped advisory lock with stale-lock recovery (sync-skills T-20260912-017 pattern), extends-stubs project as self-contained resolved personas via the scaffold-time composer (helpers/resolve-pm-stub.ts composeResolvedAgentContent — import-verified pure), exported resolvePlatformModel ports the agent-model-gate v1.2.0 claude-<alias>-<ver> → <alias> rule, and the B-PGW-pattern registry↔adapter coverage guard fails loud before any per-file work. Mirrors are project-scoped: a root without variant.json is a green no-op (design §12). `--check` is the blocking parity gate consumed by the audit arm (spawn-if-exists, the validate-model-registry precedent) and dev-sync step 4.61.
